Job Hunter Agent  – AIE314 Summer26 1. The project in one page You will build a system that takes a candidate's CV, finds real vacancies, decides which are worth applying to, rewrites the CV and cover letter for the chosen vacancy, asks a human to approve, submits the application and tracks the result. The system is delivered as five independent n8n workflows, not one. Each student owns one workflow completely — its design, code, tests, evaluation, documentation and  defence  — and the five are connected only at the end, through data contracts agreed in advance (Section 3). Three rules that shape everything Nobody waits for anybody.  Every module has a sample input file provided with this brief, so all five students start on day one and every module must run, be tested and be demonstrated alone. Every choice is justified.  Model, API, algorithm, database, threshold, weight — each chosen against declared criteria and recorded. A good choice with no reasoning scores lower than a defensible one with clear reasoning. Every claim is measured.  "It works well" is not a result. A number, its method and its limitation are. <w:left w:val="single" w:sz="18" w:space="10" w:color="E8A33D"/><w:bottom w:val="single" w:sz="2" w:space="6" w:color="F0D3A6"/><w:right w:val="single" w:sz="2" w:space="6" w:color="F0D3A6"/></w:pBdr><w:shd w:val="clear" w:color="auto" w:fill="FFF4E5"/><w:spacing w:before="120" w:after="160"/><w:ind w:left="120" w:right="120"/></w:pPr><w:r><w:rPr><w:b/><w:bCs/><w:color w:val="8A5200"/></w:rPr><w:t xml:space="preserve">How you will be examined:  In the final discussion you open your own workflow and explain any node in it. A module that runs but that its owner cannot explain is not credited to that student. system acts in a real  labour  market on a real person's behalf. Automatic submission of AI-generated documents is an ethical risk and often breaches platform terms. 3. Interface contracts These are the only things the five modules know about each other. They are frozen at Milestone 1 and change only through the procedure in 3.6. Rules Field names are  lower_snake_case  and fixed. Do not rename, abbreviate or translate them. Timestamps are ISO-8601 UTC. Identifiers ( candidate_id ,  job_id ) stay unchanged through the whole pipeline. Arrays are arrays, never comma-separated strings. Missing values are explicit nulls, never empty strings. Every payload carries  schema_version . Change the shape, bump the version. A consuming module validates its input and fails loudly. w:val="18"/></w:rPr><w:t>It runs standalone on a mock package and emits valid contract-3.5 output. Nothing is ever submitted without a recorded approval. Repeated runs of the same package never produce a duplicate submission. Every injected failure ends in a logged, recovered or explicitly failed state — never a silent stop. 5. How the five modules come together Entry condition Integration starts only when all five modules independently meet their "Done when" criteria. Integrating a half-finished module produces failures nobody can attribute to anyone. The Integration Lead The team nominates one member, in addition to their module role, to maintain the contract document, the change log and the integration workflow. It is a coordination duty, not extra ownership — the Integration Lead does not edit anyone's module. The black-box rule No student edits another student's workflow. Problems are reported at the boundary — "Module 2 emitted a record with no  application_url " — not fixed inside someone else's module. If a fix needs a contract change, it goes through 3.6. Nothing is patched quietly. Order of connection Connect in pairs, so every failure has at most one new suspect. Wiring all five at once and debugging the result is the slowest possible route. <w:tblPr><w:tblW w:w="9026" w:type="dxa"/><w:tblBorders><w:top w:val="single" w:sz="4" w:space="0" six checks on the producer's real output — not on the sample file: All required fields present, correctly named and correctly typed. schema_version  matches the agreed contract version. Identifiers stable and unique across runs. Arrays are arrays; nulls are explicit. Behaviour  on an empty result set is defined on both sides. A deliberately malformed payload is rejected with a clear error, not silently absorbed. Mechanics in n8n Complete_Job_Hunter.json    ├── Execute Workflow → Module 1         candidate_profile    ├── Execute Workflow → Module 2        jobs    ├── Execute Workflow → Module 3         ranked_jobs    ├── Loop over decision = APPLY    │      ├── Execute Workflow → Module  4   application _package    │      └── Execute Workflow → Module  5   application _status    └── Final run report Execute Workflow nodes are the default. Webhooks, HTTP Request nodes, files or a shared database are acceptable alternatives where you can say why — a module that must run on its own schedule, or a payload too large to pass inline. Whichever you use, document it in the architecture section. The end-to-end acceptance  run One complete demonstration: a real CV goes in; a profile is extracted; jobs are retrieved from at least two sources; jobs are ranked with explanations; an APPLY job is tailored into a truthful CV and cover letter; a human approves; it is submitted; the tracking store shows the record; the candidate receives a confirmation. Failures at any stage must be visible and explainable, not silent. 6. How you will be evaluated Two things carry most of the weight in this project: whether you can defend your choices, and whether you can measure your own work. This section is the method for both. 6.1 Defending a choice Use a weighted matrix wherever you pick between named alternatives. Four steps: list the criteria that actually matter for your module and say why; assign weights summing to 100% and justify them; score each option 1–5, based on measurement wherever measurement is presentation, evaluation summary 7.2 What you hand in Student_<N>_Module_<N>_< FullName >/         Job_Hunter_Agent _<Team>/ ├── workflow/module_<N>. json               ├── Module_1 … Module_5/ ├──  test_data /                            ├── Integration/ ├── outputs/                              │   ├──  Complete_Job_Hunter.json ├── evaluation/                           │   ├── interface_contract_v1.pdf │   ├──  ground_ truth .( csv| json )            │   ├── change_log.md │   └──  results.( xlsx| pdf )                 │   └── integration_test_log.pdf ├── decisions/                            ├── Architecture/ ├── screenshots/                          ├── Demo /  ( recorded run, mp4) └── documentation.pdf                     └── Final_Report.pdf documentation.pdf contains: the module's purpose and place in the system; its input/output spec with a worked example; a walkthrough of what each significant node does and why it exists; the decision records; the test plan and results including negatives; the evaluation with method, numbers, interpretation and limitations; known issues; and a short note on what went wrong during development and how you diagnosed it. 7.3 Rules AI tools.  Expected — this is a project about building with AI. Two conditions: disclose where AI helped you produce the deliverable as opposed to being a component inside the system, and own everything you submit. Anything you cannot explain is not yours for grading. Sources.  Do not scrape any site against its terms of service. If a source forbids automated access, use a different one and say why. Real applications.  Never submit to a real employer on anyone's behalf without their explicit consent. A mock endpoint costs you no marks. Personal data.  Use your own CV, a  teammate's  with permission, or a synthetic one. Never a  stranger's . Credentials.  In n8n credentials only — never inline in nodes, never in the exported workflow, redacted in screenshots. Truthfulness.  A system that improves someone's chances by misrepresenting them is a failed system, whatever its metrics say. Individual accountability.  Team marks are shared; module marks are not. A strong team cannot carry a module that does not run, and a student who did not build their module does not receive its marks.
